{
  "abstract_verbatim": "Finite prefix instructions may change area and erase information. We give explicit history processors and smooth incompressible motions that retain that information and realize every instruction on its full domain. A first application assigns each machine and finite input a smooth mean-zero force on the flat unit three-torus, at any fixed positive computable viscosity. The solution starts from rest; a fixed particle enters a fixed open strip exactly when the machine halts. The force repeats with period one after an initial loading interval. We then prove alternative realizations using full boxes, normal compensation, invariant planes, and a spatial clock. The constructions specify their initialization, comparison class, derivative bounds, and continuous-time observation. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Finite prefix instructions may change area and erase information. We give explicit history processors and smooth incompressible motions that retain that information and realize every instruction on its full domain. A first application assigns each machine and finite input a smooth mean-zero force on the flat unit three-torus, at any fixed positive computable viscosity. The solution starts from rest; a fixed particle enters a fixed open strip exactly when the machine halts. The force repeats with period one after an initial loading interval. We then prove alternative realizations using full boxes, normal compensation, invariant planes, and a spatial clock. The constructions specify their initialization, comparison class, derivative bounds, and continuous-time observation. Exact formulas and effective cutoffs provide finite descriptions of the fields and all their derivatives.

- **claim-interspersed-history-fluid-event** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-interleaved)): At fixed positive computable viscosity, every machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth mean-zero general force on the unit $\mathbb T^3$, with all mixed derivatives bounded and period one for $t\ge1$. Its zero-data solution is globally smooth and unique in the classical periodic comparison class, with uniformly bounded kinetic energy. Its material trajectory from $(1/8,1/4,1/4)$ enters $\{x:1/2<x_1<1\}$ at some finite time exactly when the machine halts. The repeated template depends only on the table, and the input enters only the loading pulse.
  - Explanation: Interspersed history retains erased instruction data while normal compensation realizes the full prefix domains. Loading and intermediate-path bounds turn the symbolic halt condition into the fixed particle event.
- **claim-pressure-gradient-orthogonality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-b-gradient)): If $w,g\in L^2(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R^3)$ satisfy $\operatorname{div}w=0$ and $\operatorname{curl}g=0$ in distributions, then $\langle w,g\rangle_{L^2}=0$. In particular, any distributional gradient $g=\nabla p\in L^2$ has zero pairing with $w$, without an assumption that $p\in L^2$.
  - Explanation: Distributional divergence and curl identities make the fields orthogonal in Fourier space. Cancellation requires integrability of the gradient, without requiring an integrable pressure potential.
- **claim-residual-realization-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-p2-realization)): Let $\nu>0$ and let $U$ be a prescribed smooth divergence-free velocity on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3$, with $U(0)=0$. On each finite interval, assume $U\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$ and $U,\nabla U$ are bounded. Put $f=\mathcal F_\nu[U]$. Then $(U,0)$ is a solution. Its velocity is unique in each of the following separately stated comparison classes, with the same force and initial data:* 1. *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v$, and a pressure representative $p\in CH^1$.* 2. *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v,\nabla v$, and an $H^1$ pressure representative at each time, with no required time continuity in that pressure norm.* 3. *Strong solutions with $v\in CH^4\cap C^1H^2$ and distributional $\nabla p\in CL^2$.* 4. *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v,\nabla v$, and a pressure representative $p\in CL^2$.* 5. *Smooth classical $v\in CH^2\cap C^1L^2$, with bounded $v,\nabla v$, without an additional integrability or normalization condition on pressure.* *The pressure gradient is unique. An $L^2$ pressure representative, when specified, is zero; otherwise pressure is determined up to a function of time. The reference material flow is a smooth diffeomorphism at every finite time and exists for every finite forward time.* *On a flat torus $\mathbb T_L^3$, the same conclusion holds in the classical class where $v,v_t$, spatial derivatives through order two, and $p,\nabla p$ are continuous on finite closed cylinders, with periodic mean-zero pressure. No Sobolev conditions at infinity are involved in that assertion.
  - Explanation: Prescribing the velocity and taking its residual proves existence by substitution. The energy comparison proves velocity uniqueness within each separately specified comparison class, with the declared pressure normalization.
- **claim-physical-coordinate-scaling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-p2-chart)): For $a,b>0$, $x=x_0+ay$, and $s=bt$, let $U(t,x)=abV(bt,(x-x_0)/a)$. Then trajectories are transformed by this change of variables, incompressibility is preserved, and at the prescribed physical viscosity $\nu$, $$\mathcal F_\nu[U](t,x)=ab^2\left[ V_s+(V\cdot\nabla_y)V-\frac{\nu}{a^2b}\Delta_yV \right](bt,(x-x_0)/a).$$
  - Explanation: The chain rule transforms trajectories and the residual while retaining the prescribed physical viscosity. Spatial rescaling changes the coefficient inside the normalized residual.
- **claim-torus-force-projection** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-projection-l2)): An effective smooth mean-zero force $g$ on $\mathbb T_L^3$ has an effective mean-zero potential $\phi$ satisfying $\Delta\phi=\operatorname{div}g$. Replacing $(g,p)$ by $(g-\nabla\phi,p-\phi)$ preserves velocity and makes the force solenoidal. Uniform mixed-derivative bounds, temporal $L^2$ bounds of their spatial suprema, and separately imposed time periodicity, eventual stationarity, or orderwise decay bounds are preserved.
  - Explanation: Solving the mean-zero Poisson equation removes the gradient part of the force. The accompanying pressure subtraction preserves velocity and the separately imposed temporal properties.
- **claim-gapped-radix-prefix-domains** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-radix)): Choose an integer $B\ge2$ and a finite digit set in $\{0,\ldots,B-2\}$ whose distinct elements differ by at least two. For words over the corresponding alphabet define $$E(v)=\sum_{j=1}^{\|v\|}d(v_j)B^{-j},\qquad I(v)=[E(v),E(v)+B^{-\|v\|}],\qquad E(L)=\sum_{j\ge1}d(L_j)B^{-j}.$$ The infinite code is injective and satisfies $E(vL)=E(v)+B^{-\|v\|}E(L)$. Incompatible finite prefixes give positively separated closed intervals. If separately disjoint state rectangles are parameterized by positive diagonal affine maps $P_s$ from $[0,1]^2$, then separately disjoint source and image cylinders give separately disjoint closed source and target rectangles $$P_s(I(v)\times I(w)),\qquad P_{s'}(I(v')\times I(w')).$$ The positive diagonal affine map between a matched pair implements the rule on every encoded configuration in its source cylinder.
  - Explanation: Digit gaps separate incompatible prefixes even at closed interval boundaries. Matching affine maps preserve the normalized tails and implement complete symbolic cylinders.
- **claim-localized-affine-curl-motion** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-curl-motion)): Let $c(t)\in\mathbb R^3$ and a positive diagonal matrix $D(t)$ be smooth effective functions on a compact interval, with $D(t_0)=I$ and $\det D(t)=1$. Put $A=\dot D D^{-1}$. Let $K_0$ be a rectangular box, allowing zero thickness in a coordinate. If an effective smooth compactly supported cutoff $\chi$ equals one on a positive collar of the moving box $K_t=c(t)+D(t)(K_0-c(t_0))$, then $$\label{eq:sa-curl} V(t,x)=\nabla\times\left\{\chi(t,x) \left[\tfrac12\dot c\times(x-c) +\tfrac13(A(x-c))\times(x-c)\right]\right\}$$ is divergence free and its trajectory from $x_0\in K_0$ is $$\label{eq:sa-affine-path} x(t)=c(t)+D(t)(x_0-c(t_0)).$$ The formula holds on an effective positive neighborhood of $K_0$. Disjoint moving supports allow finitely many such motions to be summed.
  - Explanation: A localized vector potential gives the prescribed affine generator on its plateau. Determinant preservation supplies the trace condition, and a positive collar extends exactness beyond the initial box.
- **claim-area-changing-sheet-routing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-sheet-routing)): Let $B_i$ and $C_i$, $1\le i\le N$, be two separately positively separated finite families of nondegenerate rational closed rectangles in $(0,1)^2$. Let $F_i:B_i\to C_i$ be the positive diagonal affine map, and let $z_0\in(0,1)$ be rational. There is an effective smooth divergence-free field supported in $(0,1)\times(0,1)^3$ whose time-one map agrees on a positive three-dimensional neighborhood of each $B_i\times\{z_0\}$ with a diagonal affine map restricting to the map $(x,z_0)\mapsto(F_i(x),z_0)$, with normal scale reciprocal to its planar area multiplier. On the sheet, each horizontal coordinate is constant during ascent and descent and is a convex combination of its two endpoint coordinates during the middle stage. The same construction applies in a larger bounded Euclidean region after translations and a choice of separated heights.
  - Explanation: Private heights separate simultaneous instruction motions. Reciprocal normal scaling compensates changing planar area, while the central-sheet horizontal path stays between its own endpoints.
- **claim-effective-fluid-assembly** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-realization)): Let $U$ be an effective smooth divergence-free field, $U(0)=0$, on the unit torus or with fixed compact spatial support on $\mathbb R^3$. Assume its mixed derivatives are bounded on each finite time interval. Then $$\label{eq:sa-residual} f=\partial_tU+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$$ is an effective prescribed force and $(u,p)=(U,0)$ is a global solution. Its velocity is unique in the applicable comparison classes of Lemma <a href="#lem:b-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-comparison">[lem:b-comparison]</a>; pressure is determined with exactly the normalization or additive time function specified there. Uniform mixed-derivative bounds, a time period, fixed compact support, and spatial mean zero pass from $U$ to $f$ when imposed on $U$. The material flow is defined for every finite time.
  - Explanation: Finite differentiation of the prescribed velocity yields an effective force. The residual inherits the stated support, mean, derivative and temporal properties, and the analytic comparison supplies uniqueness.
- **claim-interspersed-history-processor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-interleaved)): Starting at $M(q_0,0)$ with left stack $b_0^\infty$ and right stack the origin-tagged input followed by blanks, this table simulates every ordinary step in finitely many positive rule applications. Erasing records at a main control gives the work tape, head, and state. The origin bit gives absolute tape positions. The full source cylinders are pairwise disjoint, as are the full image cylinders. The initialized run reaches a halting main control exactly when the machine halts.
  - Explanation: Records distinguish incoming instructions on complete image cylinders. Finite scans expose the next work symbol, and erasing records recovers the machine configuration and absolute tape positions.
- **claim-full-box-normal-compensation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-boxes)): Suppose $R_i^\pm\subset\mathbb R^2$, $1\le i\le N$, $N\ge1$, are separately separated nondegenerate rational closed rectangles. Let $F_i$ be their positive diagonal affine maps, and explicitly require $F_i(R_i^-)=R_i^+$. Their factors $\lambda_{i1},\lambda_{i2}$ are positive rational numbers, given by the corresponding rational side-length ratios. Put $\lambda_{i3}=(\lambda_{i1}\lambda_{i2})^{-1}$, and choose any positive rational source half-heights $h_i^-$. Define $h_i^+=\lambda_{i3}h_i^-$. There is an effective compactly supported divergence-free unit-time pulse whose map on each full box $R_i^-\times[-h_i^-,h_i^-]$ is the specified horizontal map together with $z\mapsto\lambda_{i3}z$. It maps that box to $R_i^+\times[-h_i^+,h_i^+]$, and the formula is exact on a positive three-dimensional neighborhood. Its centers interpolate linearly in the horizontal coordinates, while every half-side is at most the larger of its endpoint half-sides. The pulse vanishes near both time endpoints.
  - Explanation: Exponential interpolation keeps each box half-side within its endpoint bounds. Separated heights permit exact determinant-preserving transport of boxes with prescribed positive thickness.
- **claim-cell-records-periodic-box-event** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-cell-records)): This recorder gives an effective smooth general force on $\mathbb R^3$ with fixed compact spatial support, period one from $t=0$, and all mixed derivatives bounded. Its zero-data solution is globally smooth with uniformly bounded energy. The particle starting at the origin has first coordinate greater than $2$ at some time exactly when the machine halts. Uniqueness holds for smooth velocities in $C_tH^2\cap C_t^1L^2$ with velocity and gradient bounded on finite intervals, and pressure in $H^1$ modulo constants at each time. Pressure is determined modulo a function of time.
  - Explanation: Input-dependent coordinate shifts put the initial code at the origin. Cell-attached histories and full-box pulses give a periodic prescription whose complete nonhalting paths avoid the detector.
- **claim-planar-parking-routing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-planar-routing)): Let $D$ be an open convex rational rectangle. Let $B_i$ and $C_i$ be two separately disjoint finite families of nondegenerate rational closed rectangles compactly contained in $D$. An effective smooth planar field, supported in $(0,1)\times D$, realizes their matched positive diagonal affine maps on positive neighborhoods of all $B_i$. If $G\subset D$ is an open convex rational rectangle, every pair $B_i,C_i\Subset G$ can have its entire controlled motion confined to $G$.
  - Explanation: Contraction, sequential parking transfers and expansion realize the matched rectangle maps. Rational clearance choices provide effective neighborhoods and optional confinement in a convex safe region.
- **claim-invariant-plane-incompressible-lift** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-invariant-lift)): Let $b(t,r)$ be an effective smooth planar field, periodic on $\mathbb T^2$ or compactly supported in $\mathbb R^2$, with bounded mixed derivatives. Let $h$ be smooth effective with bounded derivatives, $h(z_0)=0$ and $h'(z_0)=1$. Then $$\label{eq:sa-lift} U(t,r,z)=(h'(z)b_1,h'(z)b_2,-h(z)\operatorname{div}_r b)$$ is divergence free and carries exactly the planar flow on $z=z_0$. The choice $h(z)=\sin(2\pi z)/(2\pi)$ for $z_0=0$ gives a mean-zero unit-torus field. The choice $h=(z-z_0)\chi(z)$, with $\chi=1$ near $z_0$ and compact support, gives compact support on $\mathbb R^3$ when $b$ is compactly supported, or a mean-zero torus field when $h$ is supported in one vertical chart. Time periods and mixed-derivative bounds are preserved.
  - Explanation: The normal component cancels the planar divergence. The vertical profile fixes an invariant plane with the original planar motion and supplies the stated support and mean properties.
- **claim-delimiter-history-processor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-delimiter)): Starting at $(N_{q_0},\#\square^\infty,w\square^\infty)$, normal configurations have form $(N_q,L\#H\square^\infty,R)$, where $L,H$ are finite, $R$ is eventually blank, and the represented left tape is $L\square^\infty$. One machine instruction takes exactly $2\|L'\|+3$ rules, where $L'$ is the updated finite left work word. Full source and image cylinders are separately disjoint, and the initialized run reaches $N_{q_h}$ exactly when the machine halts.
  - Explanation: The updated left word is transferred, a branch-identifying history record is inserted below the delimiter, and the word is restored. Full-cylinder inverse distinctions prove separation and faithful halting.
- **claim-delimiter-invariant-plane-events** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-left-stack-plane)): For every machine and finite input, each row below gives an effective smooth mean-zero general force on the unit torus, periodic of period one for $t\ge1$, with all mixed derivatives bounded. Its zero-data solution is unique in the classical periodic class and has uniformly bounded energy. The stated fixed particle enters the stated fixed open set exactly when the machine halts.* <div class="center"> \| *Processor* \| *Initial label* \| *Open set* \| \|:--------------------------------\|:----------------\|:-------------------------------\| \| *Delimiter* \| *$(1/2,1/2,0)$* \| *$(2/3,5/6)^2\times\mathbb T$* \| \| *Delimiter, origin coordinates* \| *$(0,0,0)$* \| *$\{x:2/3<x_1<11/12\}$* \| \| *Separate marker push* \| *$(1/4,1/4,0)$* \| *$\{x:3/4<x_1<1\}$* \| </div> *The repeated pulse uses only the machine; a separate loader uses the input. All formulas allow arbitrary fixed positive real viscosity as a parameter; absolute computability uses computable viscosity.
  - Explanation: The delimiter variants use distinct coordinate placements and loaders. Planar safe-region routing and the invariant-plane lift prove each listed continuous-time event with its own fixed label and detector.
- **claim-input-insertion-prefix-processor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-prefix-insertion)): These source and image cylinders are separately disjoint. From $(S,\sharp\square^\infty,\square^\infty)$ the first procedure inserts $w$, and thereafter one ordinary step takes $2\|L'\|+3$ rules and reaches the correct main configuration. The run reaches a halt main control exactly when the machine halts.
  - Explanation: A distinguished source state inserts the input through the same prefix mechanism as later instructions. Branch records and scan guards keep the source and image cylinder families separately disjoint.
- **claim-input-insertion-periodic-force** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-prefix-insertion)): This processor gives an effective smooth general force on $\mathbb R^3$ with period one from zero, a fixed compact spatial support, and all mixed derivatives bounded. Its zero-data solution is globally smooth, has uniformly bounded energy, and is unique among smooth classical solutions with $u\in C_tH^2\cap C_t^1L^2$, $p\in C_tH^1$, and bounded velocity and gradient on each finite time interval. The particle from the origin has first coordinate greater than $2$ at some time exactly when the machine halts. The formula is effective relative to any fixed real $\nu>0$.
  - Explanation: Including initialization in the repeated table removes the separate loading interval. Full-box compensation gives compactly supported periodic forcing and an exact half-space halting event.
- **claim-finite-window-slab-event** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-delimiter-history)): This processor gives an effective smooth mean-zero force on the unit torus, periodic of period one for $t\ge1$, with every mixed derivative bounded. Its zero-data solution is globally smooth, unique in the classical periodic class, and has uniformly bounded energy. The particle from $(1/4,1/4,1/4)$ enters $\{x:1/2<x_1<3/4\}$ exactly when the machine halts. A general residual force with pressure zero or a solenoidal projected force with its accompanying pressure may be chosen; both give the same velocity. The repeated pulse depends only on the table and the loader on the input.
  - Explanation: Boundary-tested work updates adjoin blanks when the finite tape window grows. Thin-box compensation and a separate loader preserve the all-time slab exclusion; projection gives a distinct force-pressure pair with the same velocity.
- **claim-repeated-loader-quadratic-checkpoints** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-repeated-loader)): Every machine and finite input give an effective smooth mean-zero general force on the unit torus, periodic of period one from time zero. Force and velocity have all mixed derivatives bounded and vanish near integer times. Their spatial supports lie in one compact subset of the cube chart. The unique classical periodic zero-data solution is globally smooth with uniformly bounded energy. From the fixed label $(1/2,1/2,1/4)$, the event $3/4<X_1(t)<7/8$ occurs exactly when the machine halts. Whenever reached, the configuration after $k$ ordinary steps is encoded at time $(k+1)^2$.
  - Explanation: An unused image cylinder accommodates a loader in every period without collisions. Scanning the accumulated history gives the stated checkpoint times, and convex sheet motion excludes false detections.
- **claim-marked-head-plane-event** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-marked-head-plane)): The marked-head compiler gives an effective smooth mean-zero general force on the unit torus, with all mixed derivatives bounded and period one for $t\ge1$. Its unique classical periodic zero-data solution is globally smooth with bounded energy. The particle from $(1/4,1/4,1/2)$ enters $(5/8,7/8)^2\times\mathbb T$ exactly when the original machine halts. Only its loader depends on the input.
  - Explanation: The work head is marked before a history-frontier excursion and its move is completed after return. The planar realization confines nonhalting paths to the safe region and detects halting in the assigned patch.
- **claim-neighboring-cell-injective-recorder** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-neighbor-compiler)): This partial map $G$ is injective. Initialize it at $C_{q_0,0}$ with head zero, finitely specified work tape, marks zero, and blank history except for its pointer at absolute cell $a\in\{1,2\}$. At its $n$th compute-state visit the work configuration is the machine’s $n$th configuration, marks are zero, records occupy $a,\ldots,a+n-1$, and the pointer is at $T_n=a+n$. If $h_n$ is the head location, the next machine step takes exactly $2(T_n-h_n)+1$ applications of $G$. In particular, its initialized run reaches $\mathcal H$ exactly when the machine halts.
  - Explanation: The return-loop guard distinguishes frontier-entry images from continuation images on the full partial domain. The initialized frontier remains ahead of the work head, making every simulated step finite with the stated exact count.
- **claim-neighbor-recorder-fluid-realizations** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-neighbor-realizations)): The neighboring-cell recorder has the following effective zero-data forced Navier–Stokes realizations at fixed positive computable viscosity. Both have all mixed derivatives bounded, period one for $t\ge1$, and uniformly bounded kinetic energy.* 1. *On the unit torus, any tape differing from blank on a specified finite set of cells is allowed. The force is mean zero and general, and the solution is unique in the classical periodic class. The particle from $(1/16,1/16,0)$ enters $\{x:1/2<x_1<7/8\}$ exactly when the machine halts.* 2. *On $\mathbb R^3$, for a finite word starting at cell zero, the force is general with one compact spatial support uniform in time. The particle from the origin has negative first coordinate at some time exactly when the machine halts. Uniqueness holds for smooth solutions with bounded velocity on finite intervals and $u\in C_tH^2\cap C_t^1L^2$, $p\in C_tH^1$; no bound on the competing velocity gradient is required.* *The repeated field depends only on the table; only the loader uses the input. Neither force is required to decay or be solenoidal. The same formulas are effective relative to arbitrary fixed real positive viscosity.
  - Explanation: The same recorder has separate torus and compact Euclidean realizations. Their loaders, safe regions and declared comparison classes give distinct fixed-particle events without requiring force decay or solenoidality.
- **claim-marked-word-five-stage-event** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-marked-five-stage)): This recorder gives an effective smooth mean-zero general force on the unit torus, with all mixed derivatives bounded and period one for $t\ge1$. Its unique classical periodic zero-data solution is global with bounded energy. The particle from $(1/4,1/4,0)$ enters $(1/2,3/4)\times\mathbb T^2$ exactly when the machine halts. The repeated protocol uses only the table and initialization uses the input. Separately, one may choose either a solenoidal force with the same eventual periodicity, or a mean-zero force satisfying $\\|f(t)\\|_{C^m}=O((1+t)^{-1})$ for every spatial order $m$, with all mixed derivatives bounded and hence temporal $L^2$ in every $C^m$ norm. These two alternatives are separate prescriptions.
  - Explanation: Separate horizontal scaling pulses and layer transport realize the marked-word processor. The periodic projected-force option and the slowing-clock decaying-force option are separate prescriptions.
- **claim-family-box-sheet-plane-events** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sb-realizations)): For every deterministic machine and finite input, and every fixed positive computable viscosity $\nu$, each row of the following table has a finite effective prescribed force $f=\mathcal F_\nu[U]$.The initial velocity is zero. The force and velocity are smooth with all mixed derivatives bounded; the velocity has bounded kinetic energy. On $\mathbb R^3$ their spatial supports lie in one compact set independent of time, allowed to depend on the finite instance. On the unit torus both fields have spatial mean zero. Their global solution is $(u,p)=(U,0)$. On the torus it is unique in the classical periodic class. For the Euclidean rows it is unique among smooth classical solutions with $u\in C_tH^2\cap C_t^1L^2$, $p\in C_tH^1$, and bounded $u,\nabla u$ on every finite time interval; pressure representatives differing by spatial constants have the same velocity conclusion. These are the specified cases of Lemma <a href="#lem:b-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-comparison">[lem:b-comparison]</a>.* *The material trajectory from the listed fixed label $a$ enters the listed fixed open set $O$ if and only if the machine halts. In a torus row, the displayed interval is an arc of $\mathbb R/\mathbb Z$ and $O$ is that interval times $\mathbb T^2$.* <div class="center"> \| *Construction* \| *Domain and fixed label $a$* \| *Observation $O$* \| *Period one from* \| \|:---------------------------\|:---------------------------------\|:-------------------------\|:------------------\| \| **Euclidean full boxes** \| \| \| \| \| *Left-word boxes* \| *$\mathbb R^3$, $(0,0,0)$* \| *$x_1<-1$* \| *$t=1$* \| \| *Right-word boxes* \| *$\mathbb R^3$, $(0,0,0)$* \| *$x_1<-1/2$* \| *$t=1$* \| \| *Tagged storage* \| *$\mathbb R^3$, $(-4,0,0)$* \| *$(-1,2)^2\times(-1,1)$* \| *$t=1$* \| \| *Boot-window prisms* \| *$\mathbb R^3$, $(-3,0,0)$* \| *$x_1>0$* \| *$t=0$* \| \| **Reserved-loader sheets** \| \| \| \| \| *Left reserved sheets* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/2,1/2,1/4)$* \| *$(1/8,1/4)$* \| *$t=0$* \| \| *Right reserved sheets* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/2,1/8,1/4)$* \| *$(3/4,15/16)$* \| *$t=0$* \| \| **Invariant planes** \| \| \| \| \| *Frontier sine plane* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/2,1/4,0)$* \| *$(5/8,15/16)$* \| *$t=1$* \| \| *Previous-tag plane* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/8,1/4,0)$* \| *$(2/3,5/6)$* \| *$t=0$* \| \| *Shifted-start plane* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(3/4,3/4,1/2)$* \| *$(0,1/3)$* \| *$t=0$* \| \| *Separator sine plane* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/4,1/2,0)$* \| *$(1/2,1)$* \| *$t=1$* \| \| *Two-cell-frontier plane* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/8,1/8,0)$* \| *$(2/3,7/8)$* \| *$t=1$* \| \| *Zero-blank plane* \| *$\mathbb T^3$, $(1/4,1/2,1/2)$* \| *$(3/4,7/8)$* \| *$t=1$* \| </div> *For the rows beginning at $t=1$, the repeated program depends only on the machine table, while the input appears in the loading pulse. The rows beginning at zero incorporate loading into the repeated prefix table, reserve a disjoint loader image, or place the initial code at the fixed label as specified in the proof. No force in this theorem is required to be solenoidal. The separator sine-plane row additionally permits an effective mean-zero solenoidal force with the same velocity and event. For arbitrary fixed real $\nu>0$, the same algebraic formulas and conclusions hold, with numerical evaluation relative to that parameter.
  - Explanation: The listed realizations combine the proved history tables with full boxes, reserved-loader sheets or invariant planes. Each row has its own initialization and continuous-path exclusion, and the analytic classes remain domain specific.
- **claim-post-update-left-word-history** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-postlog)): The delimiter-history table of Lemma <a href="#lem:sa-delimiter" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:sa-delimiter">15</a>, with $(N_q,D_i,U_q,\star)$ renamed $(P_q,T_i,U_q,\diamond)$, applies to any finite halt set $H$ by omitting its normal rules at every $q\in H$. It simulates one machine transition in exactly $2\|L'\|+3$ instructions, with $L'$ the updated finite left word, and has separately disjoint full source and image cylinders. Starting with an empty left word, $\|L_j\|\le j$ after $j$ machine transitions.
  - Explanation: A bijective renaming transfers the delimiter processor to a finite halt set. Its complete cylinder inverse and exact instruction count are preserved, and the initialized left-word length has the stated bound.
- **claim-previous-tag-pre-update-history** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-prelog)): There is an injective partial prefix processor starting from a distinguished state $I$ with both stacks $\square^\infty$. One instruction loads the input. At a nonhalting main state with $k$ stored left cells, the next machine transition takes exactly $2k+4$ instructions.
  - Explanation: The previous incoming tag is recorded before updating the tape. A reserved boot tag separates initialization from later image cylinders, and the transfer and restoration scans give the exact count.
- **claim-right-word-blank-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-rightword)): An effective injective prefix table represents a machine configuration by $$(q,L,r\#h\square^\infty),\qquad L\in A^{\mathbb N},\quad r\in A^*,\quad \|r\|\ge1,$$ where $r$ starts at the head and all unlisted cells to its right are blank. If the local write and move leave a word $v$ before $\#$, one simulated transition uses exactly $2\|v\|+3$ instructions and replaces the right stack by $v\square\#\tau_eh\square^\infty$.
  - Explanation: The right word is scanned to its delimiter, an explicit blank and history record are appended, and the word is restored. The inserted blank makes a right move from a minimal window well defined.
- **claim-tagged-left-word-sweep** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-tagged)): There is an injective prefix processor whose update sweeps the finite left word through a disjoint tagged alphabet. Its exact transition count is $2m+3$, where $m$ is the updated left-word length.
  - Explanation: A disjoint tagged alphabet distinguishes scan traffic from ordinary work symbols. History insertion and untagging restore the work word while preserving full-cylinder injectivity.
- **claim-boot-window-prefix-table** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-window)): There is an effective injective prefix table with a boot state $s_*$ and main states $N_q$. From $(s_*,E^\infty,E^\infty)$ it loads the input in one instruction. At a main configuration $$(N_q,\ell Ja rE^\infty,\sigma g_*E^\infty)$$ it represents the finite tape window $\ell ar$, with head at $a$; both words $\ell,r$ are in left-to-right order and all cells outside the window are blank. One machine transition takes $3+\|\ell\|+\|\ell_{\rm new}\|$ instructions.
  - Explanation: A boot rule installs the finite work window and history base. Scans expose the head marker, boundary cases extend the window, and restoration records the update tag.
- **claim-separate-frontier-instruction-recorder** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-p2-rapid-history)): There is a partial one-head machine with alphabet $\Sigma=A\times\{\mathtt e,\mathtt F,\lambda_r:r\in\mathcal R\}\times\{0,1\}$, where $\mathcal R=(Q\setminus H)\times A$, and states $B_q,N_q,L_q,A_r,R_r$. With the history frontier initially at absolute cell $2$, it simulates transition number $n+1$ in $$2(2+n-h_{n+1})+4$$ auxiliary steps, where $h_{n+1}$ is the new machine head position. For every target state, all incoming rules have the same direction and the written combined symbol distinguishes their old state and scanned symbol.
  - Explanation: The explicitly identified recorder renaming preserves all guards and full-domain inverse properties. A separate frontier-advance instruction gives the stated auxiliary count and target-state incoming-direction condition.
- **claim-two-cell-frontier-update** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-neighbor)): There is an injective partial update on a centered tape with alphabet $\Sigma=A\times(\{\bot,\#\}\sqcup\mathcal R)\times\{0,1\}$ and labels $S_q,L_q,P_r,R_r$. At the $n$th main checkpoint its frontier is $e=2+n$, and the next machine transition takes exactly $2(e-h_{n+1})+2$ updates. Each branch can be specified by the three source cells $-1,0,1$ and has a whole product prefix cylinder as its image.
  - Explanation: Editing the frontier and its neighbor saves a separate advance instruction. The return guard proves injectivity outside initialized runs, and the complete local window yields whole product image cylinders.
- **claim-exact-code-conjugacy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-code-equivalence)): If two encodings use the same prefix table, possibly after a bijective renaming of letters and states, the map that sends each encoded state and pair of words to its other encoding conjugates the partial updates on their entire coded domains. On every branch, both extensions in <a href="#eq:sb-coded-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:sb-coded-map">[eq:sb-coded-map]</a> retain precisely the two tail coordinates. Changes of base, digits, or state rectangles require no identification of the surrounding fluid motions.
  - Explanation: Injective encodings of the same words conjugate the partial symbolic updates. This equivalence preserves tails on each branch and does not identify the surrounding interpolation fields.
- **claim-bounded-observer-storage-routing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sb-storage)): Suppose the horizontal source and target rectangles for a prefix table lie in state bands $[o_s,o_s+1]\times[0,1]$, where the unique halting band has $o_h=0$ and every other band has $o_s\ge3$. Give source boxes half-height $$h=\frac1{1+\sum_j\lambda_{j3}}$$ and target boxes half-height $h\lambda_{j3}$. An effective divergence-free pulse implements their determinant-one affine maps in $7N$ slots. Every trajectory from a box whose source and target bands are nonhalting avoids $\mathcal O=(-1,2)\times(-1,2)\times(-1,1)$ throughout the pulse.
  - Explanation: All source boxes are evacuated before any target is occupied. Scaling at private storage positions and high horizontal travel preserve volume and avoid the bounded observer for nonhalting branches.
- **claim-logarithmic-clock-decaying-alternatives** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-sb-log)): For each of the left-word-box, right-word-box, and tagged-storage constructions, retain its fixed initial label and observation set. There is also an effective smooth force of fixed compact spatial support in $L^2([0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3)$ with exactly the same halting event and zero initial velocity. It and all its derivatives are bounded. The velocity’s spatial derivatives are $O((1+t)^{-1})$, and their first time derivatives are $O((1+t)^{-2})$, uniformly in space. The force itself is $O((1+t)^{-1})$ uniformly in space. The viscosity in this statement is the same fixed physical $\nu$ as before the time change.
  - Explanation: An increasing onto logarithmic clock traverses the same material path at a slower speed. Recomputing the residual with the same viscosity supplies decay and finite space-time square integral while retaining the event.
- **claim-reciprocal-compact-periodic-realizations** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-p2-rapid-periodic)): For every deterministic machine and finite input, and every fixed computable viscosity $\nu>0$, there are effective smooth velocities $U$ and forces $f$ on $\mathbb R^3$ with the following properties. Both have all mixed derivatives bounded, support in a compact spatial set independent of time, and period one for $t\ge1$; $U(0)=0$. The solution with pressure zero is unique in the class $$u\in C([0,T];H^2)\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\qquad p\in C([0,T];H^1),\qquad \sup_{[0,T]\times\mathbb R^3}(\|u\|+\|\nabla u\|)<\infty$$ for every finite $T$, and its kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. The following are two separate constructions with their stated fixed particle events:* 1. *Four successive stationary localized fields, with initial label $(-1,0,0)$ and detector $\{x_1>0\}$.* 2. *Three successive phases of moving full-box fields, with initial label $(0,0,0)$ and detector $\{x_1<-1\}$.* *In each case the particle enters the detector at some real time if and only if the machine halts. The repeated part of the prescription depends only on the machine; the input appears only in a one-time loading pulse.
  - Explanation: Reciprocal planar scales eliminate the need for normal compensation. Stationary localized fields and moving full boxes give separate compact implementations with different fixed labels and half-space observers.
- **claim-spatial-clock-decaying-fluid-event** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-sa-autonomous)): At fixed positive computable viscosity, a deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth mean-zero general force on the unit torus such that, for every $k\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\beta$, $$\label{eq:sa-autonomous-force-class} \\|\partial_t^k\partial_x^\beta f(t)\\|_\infty \le C_{k,\beta}(1+t)^{-1}.$$ Its zero-data Navier–Stokes solution is globally smooth and unique in the classical periodic comparison class, with uniformly bounded kinetic energy and mean-zero pressure. For the fixed label and open set $$\label{eq:sa-autonomous-event} y_*=(1/2,1/2,1/4),\qquad \mathcal O=\{(x_1,x_2,z):1/2<x_1<1,\ 0<z<1/8\},$$ its material trajectory enters $\mathcal O$ exactly when the machine halts. Samples encode the full machine computation through the first halt, or indefinitely if there is none. The prescription uses only the finite table and input; arbitrary fixed real positive viscosity is allowed as a relative coefficient.
  - Explanation: A Hamiltonian transition pulse is suspended in a spatial clock. The quiet phase window excludes transient false positives, and a scalar profile starts the fluid from rest while covering all internal time and producing decay.
- **claim-hamiltonian-full-rectangle-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-sa-autonomous-extension)): Let $U_i,\widehat U_i$, $1\le i\le J$, $J\ge1$, be two separately disjoint families of nondegenerate rational closed rectangles in $\mathbb R^2$. For each pair prescribe $$X\mapsto\widehat c_i+ \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_i,\lambda_i^{-1})(X-c_i),\qquad\lambda_i>0$$ with rational $\lambda_i$ and the respective centers, and require the compatibility condition $$\widehat U_i=\widehat c_i+ \operatorname{diag}(\lambda_i,\lambda_i^{-1})(U_i-c_i).$$ There is an effective smooth compactly supported planar Hamiltonian field $V(\xi,X)$, with time support in $(1/4,3/4)$, whose unit-time map has this formula on each $U_i$ and a positive neighborhood. The construction gives a rational bound on its spatial support and all controlled rectangle paths.
  - Explanation: Magnified center separation and distant parking permit reciprocal deformation without shrinking area. Rational obstacle detours and localized Hamiltonians realize the compatible maps on complete rectangles and positive neighborhoods.

If an effective smooth compactly supported cutoff $\\chi$ equals one on a positive collar of the moving box $K_t=c(t)+D(t)(K_0-c(t_0))$, then $$\\label{eq:sa-curl}\n V(t,x)=\\nabla\\times\\left\\{\\chi(t,x)\n \\left[\\tfrac12\\dot c\\times(x-c)\n       +\\tfrac13(A(x-c))\\times(x-c)\\right]\\right\\}$$ is divergence free and its trajectory from $x_0\\in K_0$ is $$\\label{eq:sa-affine-path}\n x(t)=c(t)+D(t)(x_0-c(t_0)).$$ The formula holds on an effective positive neighborhood of $K_0$.           "exact_quote": "Let $c(t)\\in\\mathbb R^3$ and a positive diagonal matrix $D(t)$ be smooth effective functions on a compact interval, with $D(t_0)=I$ and $\\det D(t)=1$. Put $A=\\dot D D^{-1}$. Let $K_0$ be a rectangular box, allowing zero thickness in a coordinate. If an effective smooth compactly supported cutoff $\\chi$ equals one on a positive collar of the moving box $K_t=c(t)+D(t)(K_0-c(t_0))$, then $$\\label{eq:sa-curl}\n V(t,x)=\\nabla\\times\\left\\{\\chi(t,x)\n \\left[\\tfrac12\\dot c\\times(x-c)\n       +\\tfrac13(A(x-c))\\times(x-c)\\right]\\right\\}$$ is divergence free and its trajectory from $x_0\\in K_0$ is $$\\label{eq:sa-affine-path}\n x(t)=c(t)+D(t)(x_0-c(t_0)).$$ The formula holds on an effective positive neighborhood of $K_0$. Disjoint moving supports allow finitely many such motions to be summed.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.5"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-sheet-routing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B_i$ and $C_i$, $1\\le i\\le N$, be two separately positively separated finite families of nondegenerate rational closed rectangles in $(0,1)^2$. Let $F_i:B_i\\to C_i$ be the positive diagonal affine map, and let $z_0\\in(0,1)$ be rational. There is an effective smooth divergence-free field supported in $(0,1)\\times(0,1)^3$ whose time-one map agrees on a positive three-dimensional neighborhood of each $B_i\\times\\{z_0\\}$ with a diagonal affine map restricting to the map $(x,z_0)\\mapsto(F_i(x),z_0)$, with normal scale reciprocal to its planar area multiplier. On the sheet, each horizontal coordinate is constant during ascent and descent and is a convex combination of its two endpoint coordinates during the middle stage. The same construction applies in a larger bounded Euclidean region after translations and a choice of separated heights.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be an effective smooth divergence-free field, $U(0)=0$, on the unit torus or with fixed compact spatial support on $\\mathbb R^3$. Assume its mixed derivatives are bounded on each finite time interval. Then $$\\label{eq:sa-residual}\n f=\\partial_tU+(U\\cdot\\nabla)U-\\nu\\Delta U$$ is an effective prescribed force and $(u,p)=(U,0)$ is a global solution. Its velocity is unique in the applicable comparison classes of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:b-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:b-comparison\">[lem:b-comparison]</a>; pressure is determined with exactly the normalization or additive time function specified there. Uniform mixed-derivative bounds, a time period, fixed compact support, and spatial mean zero pass from $U$ to $f$ when imposed on $U$. The material flow is defined for every finite time.",

        "exact_quote": "Starting at $M(q_0,0)$ with left stack $b_0^\\infty$ and right stack the origin-tagged input followed by blanks, this table simulates every ordinary step in finitely many positive rule applications.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-sb-boxes",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"lem:sa-full-box-layers\" label=\"lem:sa-full-box-layers\"></span>Suppose $R_i^\\pm\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, $1\\le i\\le N$, $N\\ge1$, are separately separated nondegenerate rational closed rectangles. Let $F_i$ be their positive diagonal affine maps, and explicitly require $F_i(R_i^-)=R_i^+$. Their factors $\\lambda_{i1},\\lambda_{i2}$ are positive rational numbers, given by the corresponding rational side-length ratios. Put $\\lambda_{i3}=(\\lambda_{i1}\\lambda_{i2})^{-1}$, and choose any positive rational source half-heights $h_i^-$. Define $h_i^+=\\lambda_{i3}h_i^-$. There is an effective compactly supported divergence-free unit-time pulse whose map on each full box $R_i^-\\times[-h_i^-,h_i^-]$ is the specified horizontal map together with $z\\mapsto\\lambda_{i3}z$. It maps that box to $R_i^+\\times[-h_i^+,h_i^+]$, and the formula is exact on a positive three-dimensional neighborhood. Its centers interpolate linearly in the horizontal coordinates, while every half-side is at most the larger of its endpoint half-sides. The pulse vanishes near both time endpoints.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-sa-cell-records",
        "exact_quote": "This recorder gives an effective smooth general force on $\\mathbb R^3$ with fixed compact spatial support, period one from $t=0$, and all mixed derivatives bounded. Its zero-data solution is globally smooth with uniformly bounded energy. The particle starting at the origin has first coordinate greater than $2$ at some time exactly when the machine halts. Uniqueness holds for smooth velocities in $C_tH^2\\cap C_t^1L^2$ with velocity and gradient bounded on finite intervals, and pressure in $H^1$ modulo constants at each time. Pressure is determined modulo a function of time.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-planar-routing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be an open convex rational rectangle. Let $B_i$ and $C_i$ be two separately disjoint finite families of nondegenerate rational closed rectangles compactly contained in $D$. An effective smooth planar field, supported in $(0,1)\\times D$, realizes their matched positive diagonal affine maps on positive neighborhoods of all $B_i$. If $G\\subset D$ is an open convex rational rectangle, every pair $B_i,C_i\\Subset G$ can have its entire controlled motion confined to $G$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-invariant-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b(t,r)$ be an effective smooth planar field, periodic on $\\mathbb T^2$ or compactly supported in $\\mathbb R^2$, with bounded mixed derivatives. Let $h$ be smooth effective with bounded derivatives, $h(z_0)=0$ and $h'(z_0)=1$. Then $$\\label{eq:sa-lift}\n U(t,r,z)=(h'(z)b_1,h'(z)b_2,-h(z)\\operatorname{div}_r b)$$ is divergence free and carries exactly the planar flow on $z=z_0$. The choice $h(z)=\\sin(2\\pi z)/(2\\pi)$ for $z_0=0$ gives a mean-zero unit-torus field. The choice $h=(z-z_0)\\chi(z)$, with $\\chi=1$ near $z_0$ and compact support, gives compact support on $\\mathbb R^3$ when $b$ is compactly supported, or a mean-zero torus field when $h$ is supported in one vertical chart. Time periods and mixed-derivative bounds are preserved.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-invariant-lift",
          "exact_quote": "Let $b(t,r)$ be an effective smooth planar field, periodic on $\\mathbb T^2$ or compactly supported in $\\mathbb R^2$, with bounded mixed derivatives. Let $h$ be smooth effective with bounded derivatives, $h(z_0)=0$ and $h'(z_0)=1$. Then $$\\label{eq:sa-lift}\n U(t,r,z)=(h'(z)b_1,h'(z)b_2,-h(z)\\operatorname{div}_r b)$$ is divergence free and carries exactly the planar flow on $z=z_0$. The choice $h(z)=\\sin(2\\pi z)/(2\\pi)$ for $z_0=0$ gives a mean-zero unit-torus field. The choice $h=(z-z_0)\\chi(z)$, with $\\chi=1$ near $z_0$ and compact support, gives compact support on $\\mathbb R^3$ when $b$ is compactly supported, or a mean-zero torus field when $h$ is supported in one vertical chart. Time periods and mixed-derivative bounds are preserved.",

Then $$\\label{eq:sa-lift}\n U(t,r,z)=(h'(z)b_1,h'(z)b_2,-h(z)\\operatorname{div}_r b)$$ is divergence free and carries exactly the planar flow on $z=z_0$. The choice $h(z)=\\sin(2\\pi z)/(2\\pi)$ for $z_0=0$ gives a mean-zero unit-torus field.         "exact_quote": "This partial map $G$ is injective. Initialize it at $C_{q_0,0}$ with head zero, finitely specified work tape, marks zero, and blank history except for its pointer at absolute cell $a\\in\\{1,2\\}$. At its $n$th compute-state visit the work configuration is the machine’s $n$th configuration, marks are zero, records occupy $a,\\ldots,a+n-1$, and the pointer is at $T_n=a+n$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-sa-neighbor-realizations",
        "exact_quote": "The neighboring-cell recorder has the following effective zero-data forced Navier–Stokes realizations at fixed positive computable viscosity. Both have all mixed derivatives bounded, period one for $t\\ge1$, and uniformly bounded kinetic energy.*\n\n1.  *On the unit torus, any tape differing from blank on a specified finite set of cells is allowed. The force is mean zero and general, and the solution is unique in the classical periodic class. The particle from $(1/16,1/16,0)$ enters $\\{x:1/2<x_1<7/8\\}$ exactly when the machine halts.*\n\n2.  *On $\\mathbb R^3$, for a finite word starting at cell zero, the force is general with one compact spatial support uniform in time. The particle from the origin has negative first coordinate at some time exactly when the machine halts. Uniqueness holds for smooth solutions with bounded velocity on finite intervals and $u\\in C_tH^2\\cap C_t^1L^2$, $p\\in C_tH^1$; no bound on the competing velocity gradient is required.*\n\n*The repeated field depends only on the table; only the loader uses the input. Neither force is required to decay or be solenoidal. The same formulas are effective relative to arbitrary fixed real positive viscosity.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-sa-marked-five-stage",
        "exact_quote": "This recorder gives an effective smooth mean-zero general force on the unit torus, with all mixed derivatives bounded and period one for $t\\ge1$. Its unique classical periodic zero-data solution is global with bounded energy. The particle from $(1/4,1/4,0)$ enters $(1/2,3/4)\\times\\mathbb T^2$ exactly when the machine halts. The repeated protocol uses only the table and initialization uses the input. Separately, one may choose either a solenoidal force with the same eventual periodicity, or a mean-zero force satisfying $\\|f(t)\\|_{C^m}=O((1+t)^{-1})$ for every spatial order $m$, with all mixed derivatives bounded and hence temporal $L^2$ in every $C^m$ norm. These two alternatives are separate prescriptions.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.5"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-sb-realizations",
        "exact_quote": "For every deterministic machine and finite input, and every fixed positive computable viscosity $\\nu$, each row of the following table has a finite effective prescribed force $f=\\mathcal F_\\nu[U]$.The initial velocity is zero. The force and velocity are smooth with all mixed derivatives bounded; the velocity has bounded kinetic energy. On $\\mathbb R^3$ their spatial supports lie in one compact set independent of time, allowed to depend on the finite instance. On the unit torus both fields have spatial mean zero. Their global solution is $(u,p)=(U,0)$. On the torus it is unique in the classical periodic class. For the Euclidean rows it is unique among smooth classical solutions with $u\\in C_tH^2\\cap C_t^1L^2$, $p\\in C_tH^1$, and bounded $u,\\nabla u$ on every finite time interval; pressure representatives differing by spatial constants have the same velocity conclusion. These are the specified cases of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:b-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:b-comparison\">[lem:b-comparison]</a>.*\n\n*The material trajectory from the listed fixed label $a$ enters the listed fixed open set $O$ if and only if the machine halts. In a torus row, the displayed interval is an arc of $\\mathbb R/\\mathbb Z$ and $O$ is that interval times $\\mathbb T^2$.*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n| *Construction*             | *Domain and fixed label $a$*     | *Observation $O$*        | *Period one from* |\n|:---------------------------|:---------------------------------|:-------------------------|:------------------|\n| **Euclidean full boxes**   |                                  |                          |                   |\n| *Left-word boxes*          | *$\\mathbb R^3$, $(0,0,0)$*       | *$x_1<-1$*               | *$t=1$*           |\n| *Right-word boxes*         | *$\\mathbb R^3$, $(0,0,0)$*       | *$x_1<-1/2$*             | *$t=1$*           |\n| *Tagged storage*           | *$\\mathbb R^3$, $(-4,0,0)$*      | *$(-1,2)^2\\times(-1,1)$* | *$t=1$*           |\n| *Boot-window prisms*       | *$\\mathbb R^3$, $(-3,0,0)$*      | *$x_1>0$*                | *$t=0$*           |\n| **Reserved-loader sheets** |                                  |                          |                   |\n| *Left reserved sheets*     | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/2,1/2,1/4)$* | *$(1/8,1/4)$*            | *$t=0$*           |\n| *Right reserved sheets*    | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/2,1/8,1/4)$* | *$(3/4,15/16)$*          | *$t=0$*           |\n| **Invariant planes**       |                                  |                          |                   |\n| *Frontier sine plane*      | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/2,1/4,0)$*   | *$(5/8,15/16)$*          | *$t=1$*           |\n| *Previous-tag plane*       | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/8,1/4,0)$*   | *$(2/3,5/6)$*            | *$t=0$*           |\n| *Shifted-start plane*      | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(3/4,3/4,1/2)$* | *$(0,1/3)$*              | *$t=0$*           |\n| *Separator sine plane*     | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/4,1/2,0)$*   | *$(1/2,1)$*              | *$t=1$*           |\n| *Two-cell-frontier plane*  | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/8,1/8,0)$*   | *$(2/3,7/8)$*            | *$t=1$*           |\n| *Zero-blank plane*         | *$\\mathbb T^3$, $(1/4,1/2,1/2)$* | *$(3/4,7/8)$*            | *$t=1$*           |\n\n</div>\n\n*For the rows beginning at $t=1$, the repeated program depends only on the machine table, while the input appears in the loading pulse. The rows beginning at zero incorporate loading into the repeated prefix table, reserve a disjoint loader image, or place the initial code at the fixed label as specified in the proof. No force in this theorem is required to be solenoidal. The separator sine-plane row additionally permits an effective mean-zero solenoidal force with the same velocity and event. For arbitrary fixed real $\\nu>0$, the same algebraic formulas and conclusions hold, with numerical evaluation relative to that parameter.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.5"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sb-postlog",
        "exact_quote": "The delimiter-history table of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:sa-delimiter\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:sa-delimiter\">15</a>, with $(N_q,D_i,U_q,\\star)$ renamed $(P_q,T_i,U_q,\\diamond)$, applies to any finite halt set $H$ by omitting its normal rules at every $q\\in H$. It simulates one machine transition in exactly $2|L'|+3$ instructions, with $L'$ the updated finite left word, and has separately disjoint full source and image cylinders.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-sb-log",
        "exact_quote": "For each of the left-word-box, right-word-box, and tagged-storage constructions, retain its fixed initial label and observation set. There is also an effective smooth force of fixed compact spatial support in $L^2([0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R^3)$ with exactly the same halting event and zero initial velocity. It and all its derivatives are bounded. The velocity’s spatial derivatives are $O((1+t)^{-1})$, and their first time derivatives are $O((1+t)^{-2})$, uniformly in space.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-p2-rapid-periodic",
        "exact_quote": "For every deterministic machine and finite input, and every fixed computable viscosity $\\nu>0$, there are effective smooth velocities $U$ and forces $f$ on $\\mathbb R^3$ with the following properties. Both have all mixed derivatives bounded, support in a compact spatial set independent of time, and period one for $t\\ge1$; $U(0)=0$. The solution with pressure zero is unique in the class $$u\\in C([0,T];H^2)\\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\\qquad\n p\\in C([0,T];H^1),\\qquad\n \\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R^3}(|u|+|\\nabla u|)<\\infty$$ for every finite $T$, and its kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. The following are two separate constructions with their stated fixed particle events:*\n\n1.  *Four successive stationary localized fields, with initial label $(-1,0,0)$ and detector $\\{x_1>0\\}$.*\n\n2.  *Three successive phases of moving full-box fields, with initial label $(0,0,0)$ and detector $\\{x_1<-1\\}$.*\n\n*In each case the particle enters the detector at some real time if and only if the machine halts. The repeated part of the prescription depends only on the machine; the input appears only in a one-time loading pulse.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The following are two separate constructions with their stated fixed particle events:*\n\n1.  *Four successive stationary localized fields, with initial label $(-1,0,0)$ and detector $\\{x_1>0\\}$.*\n\n2.  *Three successive phases of moving full-box fields, with initial label $(0,0,0)$ and detector $\\{x_1<-1\\}$.*\n\n*In each case the particle enters the detector at some real time if and only if the machine halts. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **stack** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A stack is an infinite word whose first letter is its accessible end.
- **prefix instruction** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A prefix instruction has the form $$(s;v,w)\longmapsto(s';v',w'),$$ meaning that it sends $(s,vL,wR)$ to $(s',v'L,w'R)$ for every pair of unchanged tails $L,R$.
- **finite effective force prescription** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A finite effective force prescription is a finite program that evaluates $f$ and any requested mixed derivative at computably specified arguments to any positive rational error, and supplies the derivative bounds used on compact sets.
- **classical periodic comparison class** ([source](#pm-h-3)): On a torus our classical comparison class requires $u,u_t$, spatial derivatives of $u$ through order two, and $p,\nabla p$ to be continuous on every finite closed time cylinder.
- **Navier–Stokes residual** ([source](#sec:model)): Write $\mathcal F_\nu[U]=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$.
- **finite-interval Sobolev continuity notation** ([source](#sec:model)): On $[0,T]$, write $CH^k=C([0,T];H^k(\mathbb R^3))$ and $C^1L^2=C^1([0,T];L^2(\mathbb R^3))$.
- **prefix cylinder** ([source](#subsec:sa-local)): A *cylinder* is the set specified by the state and the two finite prefixes.
- **gapped radix codes and prefix intervals** ([source](#stmt-lem-sa-radix)): For words over the corresponding alphabet define $$E(v)=\sum_{j=1}^{\|v\|}d(v_j)B^{-j},\qquad I(v)=[E(v),E(v)+B^{-\|v\|}],\qquad E(L)=\sum_{j\ge1}d(L_j)B^{-j}.$$
- **flat cutoff profiles** ([source](#eq:p2-step)): For later cutoff formulas put $$\label{eq:p2-step} \rho(s)=\begin{cases}e^{-1/s},&s>0,\\0,&s\le0,\end{cases} \qquad \sigma(s)=\frac{\rho(s)}{\rho(s)+\rho(1-s)}.$$
- **incompatible prefixes** ([source](#sec:sb-prefix)): Two prefixes are incompatible if they differ at a position specified by both.
- **invariant-plane lift** ([source](#stmt-lem-sa-invariant-lift)): Then $$\label{eq:sa-lift} U(t,r,z)=(h'(z)b_1,h'(z)b_2,-h(z)\operatorname{div}_r b)$$ is divergence free and carries exactly the planar flow on $z=z_0$.
- **autonomous velocity profile** ([source](#eq:sa-autonomous-profile)): Use the scalar profile $$\label{eq:sa-autonomous-profile} A_0(t)=\frac{t}{(1+t)^2}=(1+t)^{-1}-(1+t)^{-2}$$

| q-neighbor-recorder-step-count | Applications of $G$ for the next machine step | = | $2(T_n-h_n)+1$ | The frontier and head at a compute-state visit. |
| q-previous-tag-instruction-count | Primitive instructions for the next machine transition | = | $2k+4$ | The stored left-cell count at the current main state. |
| q-right-word-instruction-count | Instructions for a simulated transition | = | $2\|v\|+3$ | The finite word left before the delimiter by the local update. |
| q-tagged-sweep-instruction-count | Instructions for a tagged-sweep transition | = | $2m+3$ | The updated left-word length. |
| q-window-transition-instruction-count | Instructions for a window-processor transition | = | $3+\|\ell\|+\|\ell_{\rm new}\|$ | The old and restored left-of-head words. |
| q-separate-frontier-auxiliary-count | Auxiliary steps for transition number $n+1$ | = | $2(2+n-h_{n+1})+4$ | The transition index and new work-head position. |
| q-two-cell-frontier-update-count | Updates for the next machine transition | = | $2(e-h_{n+1})+2$ | The current frontier and new work-head position. |
| q-storage-routing-slots | Slots in the storage and delivery pulse | = | $7N$ | The finite instruction family. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-gradient-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-b-gradient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-b-gradient): Fourier orthogonality and Plancherel. -> Pressure-gradient pairing vanishes.
- **step-residual-energy-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-p2-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-p2-realization): Substitution, cutoff integration by parts and energy comparison. -> Existence, class-specific uniqueness, pressure normalization and finite-time material flow.
- **step-physical-scaling** [claim](#stmt-lem-p2-chart) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-p2-chart): Chain rule at fixed physical viscosity. -> Physical residual formula and transformed incompressible trajectories.
- **step-torus-poisson-projection** [claim](#stmt-prop-projection-l2) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-projection-l2): Fourier multipliers with effective summable derivative tails. -> Solenoidal force and accompanying pressure with preserved temporal contracts.
- **step-gapped-radix-rectangles** [claim](#stmt-lem-sa-radix) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sa-radix): Nested gapped digit intervals and normalized tail preservation. -> Injective codes and separated complete branch rectangles.
- **step-affine-curl-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-sa-curl-motion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sa-curl-motion): Localized curl potential and plateau ODE uniqueness. -> Exact affine motion on boxes and positive neighborhoods.
- **step-normal-compensated-sheets** [claim](#stmt-lem-sa-sheet-routing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sa-sheet-routing): Lift to private heights, interpolate widths with reciprocal normal scale, then lower. -> Exact sheet maps with endpoint-convex horizontal paths.
- **step-effective-fluid-residual** [claim](#stmt-lem-sa-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sa-realization): Finite differentiation of the velocity and residual realization. -> Effective force inheriting the imposed field properties.
- **step-interspersed-symbolic-history** [claim](#stmt-lem-sa-interleaved) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sa-interleaved): Tape-erasure invariant, terminating scans and full-cylinder inverse distinctions. -> Injective initialized simulation with exact halting correspondence.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-gapped-code**: Gapped digits give injective stack codes, and prefixing preserves the normalized tail coordinate.
- **invariant-prefix-affine-action**: The matched positive diagonal affine map implements the prefix replacement on every encoded configuration in its source cylinder.
- **invariant-normal-compensation**: The reciprocal normal scale compensates the product of the horizontal scales.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-uniform-precision**: Treat an exact coded halting event as a uniform finite-precision detector. -> The positional representation does not supply a uniform tolerance for the complete computation.
- **anti-initialized-only-injectivity**: Extend a prefix program after checking inverse uniqueness only on its initialized run. -> Other tails can produce overlapping branch images and invalidate the required invertible extension.
- **anti-endpoint-only-observation**: Infer detector avoidance from safe instruction endpoints. -> An intermediate transport path can enter the observer.
- **anti-invariant-neighborhood**: Treat the positive neighborhood supplied by an individual box motion as an invariant neighborhood for the repeated program. -> A preceding target thickness need not coincide with the next source thickness.
- **anti-finite-check-global-certificate**: Use finite exact prefix tests to certify the global fluid halting equivalence. -> Finite arithmetic tests do not verify the smooth field, comparison uniqueness, or all intermediate material times.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The gapped radix and prefix-affine formulas give a finite constructive arithmetic subresult for finite prefixes and eventually constant tails. The executable certificate checks these identities and reciprocal normal compensation on explicit inputs; it does not certify infinite computations or the analytic fluid realization.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with base, digits, blank, source_left, source_right, target_left, target_right, tail_left, tail_right.
# PRECONDITIONS: Integer base at least two; distinct digits in the permitted range with gaps at least two; all words and blank use those digits.
# OUTPUTS: JSON array of exact rational strings for the mapped left code, mapped right code, and reciprocal normal scale.
# TIME: Linear in the number of supplied digits, apart from exact integer arithmetic costs.
# SPACE: Linear in the input length and the exact integer representations.
# ARITHMETIC: Arbitrary precision integers and exact rational fractions.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces a nonzero exit.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

p = json.load(sys.stdin)
B = p['base']
digits = p['digits']
blank = p['blank']
keys = ['source_left', 'source_right', 'target_left', 'target_right', 'tail_left', 'tail_right']
assert type(B) is int and B >= 2
assert isinstance(digits, list) and digits
assert all(type(d) is int and 0 <= d <= B - 2 for d in digits)
ordered = sorted(digits)
assert all(b - a >= 2 for a, b in zip(ordered, ordered[1:]))
assert type(blank) is int and blank in digits
for key in keys:
    assert isinstance(p[key], list)
    assert all(type(d) is int and d in digits for d in p[key])

def prefix(word):
    value = Fraction(0)
    for d in reversed(word):
        value = (value + d) / B
    return value

def tail(word):
    return prefix(word) + Fraction(blank, B - 1) / (B ** len(word))

def mapped(src, dst, suffix):
    initial = prefix(src) + tail(suffix) / (B ** len(src))
    scale = Fraction(B) ** (len(src) - len(dst))
    return prefix(dst) + scale * (initial - prefix(src))

x = mapped(p['source_left'], p['target_left'], p['tail_left'])
y = mapped(p['source_right'], p['target_right'], p['tail_right'])
source_total = len(p['source_left']) + len(p['source_right'])
target_total = len(p['target_left']) + len(p['target_right'])
normal = Fraction(B) ** (target_total - source_total)
print(json.dumps([str(v.numerator) + '/' + str(v.denominator) for v in [x, y, normal]]))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\nwith open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n    p = json.load(stream)\nwith open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n    candidate = json.load(stream)\nexpected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\nB = p['base']\nassert type(B) is int and B >= 2\nalphabet = p['digits']\nassert isinstance(alphabet, list) and alphabet\nassert all(type(d) is int and 0 <= d <= B - 2 for d in alphabet)\nassert len(set(alphabet)) == len(alphabet)\nassert all(abs(a - b) >= 2 for i, a in enumerate(alphabet) for b in alphabet[i + 1:])\nassert type(p['blank']) is int and p['blank'] in alphabet\nfor key in ['source_left', 'source_right', 'target_left', 'target_right', 'tail_left', 'tail_right']:\n    assert isinstance(p[key], list)\n    assert all(type(d) is int and d in alphabet for d in p[key])\n\ndef encode_replacement(side):\n    word = p['target_' + side] + p['tail_' + side]\n    denominator = 1\n    numerator = 0\n    for digit in word:\n        numerator = numerator * B + digit\n        denominator *= B\n    # Sum the constant infinite suffix as a geometric series.\n    return Fraction(numerator * (B - 1) + p['blank'], denominator * (B - 1))\n\nhorizontal_product = Fraction(1)\nfor side in ['left', 'right']:\n    for unused in p['source_' + side]:\n        horizontal_product *= B\n    for unused in p['target_' + side]:\n        horizontal_product /= B\ntruth = [encode_replacement('left'), encode_replacement('right'), 1 / horizontal_product]\n\ndef decode(values):\n    assert isinstance(values, list) and len(values) == 3\n    assert all(isinstance(value, str) for value in values)\n    return [Fraction(value) for value in values]\n\nassert decode(expected) == truth\nassert decode(candidate) == truth\n",

Acceptance requires exact agreement with independently encoded replacement words and the supplied expected result.",

The executable certificate checks these identities and reciprocal normal compensation on explicit inputs; it does not certify infinite computations or the analytic fluid realization.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with base, digits, blank, source_left, source_right, target_left, target_right, tail_left, tail_right.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Integer base at least two; distinct digits in the permitted range with gaps at least two; all words and blank use those digits.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON array of exact rational strings for the mapped left code, mapped right code, and reciprocal normal scale.\n# TIME: Linear in the number of supplied digits, apart from exact integer arithmetic costs.\n# SPACE: Linear in the input length and the exact integer representations.\n# ARITHMETIC: Arbitrary precision integers and exact rational fractions.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and produces a nonzero exit.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\np = json.load(sys.stdin)\nB = p['base']\ndigits = p['digits']\nblank = p['blank']\nkeys = ['source_left', 'source_right', 'target_left', 'target_right', 'tail_left', 'tail_right']\nassert type(B) is int and B >= 2\nassert isinstance(digits, list) and digits\nassert all(type(d) is int and 0 <= d <= B - 2 for d in digits)\nordered = sorted(digits)\nassert all(b - a >= 2 for a, b in zip(ordered, ordered[1:]))\nassert type(blank) is int and blank in digits\nfor key in keys:\n    assert isinstance(p[key], list)\n    assert all(type(d) is int and d in digits for d in p[key])\n\ndef prefix(word):\n    value = Fraction(0)\n    for d in reversed(word):\n        value = (value + d) / B\n    return value\n\ndef tail(word):\n    return prefix(word) + Fraction(blank, B - 1) / (B ** len(word))\n\ndef mapped(src, dst, suffix):\n    initial = prefix(src) + tail(suffix) / (B ** len(src))\n    scale = Fraction(B) ** (len(src) - len(dst))\n    return prefix(dst) + scale * (initial - prefix(src))\n\nx = mapped(p['source_left'], p['target_left'], p['tail_left'])\ny = mapped(p['source_right'], p['target_right'], p['tail_right'])\nsource_total = len(p['source_left']) + len(p['source_right'])\ntarget_total = len(p['target_left']) + len(p['target_right'])\nnormal = Fraction(B) ** (target_total - source_total)\nprint(json.dumps([str(v.numerator) + '/' + str(v.denominator) for v in [x, y, normal]]))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The delimiter-history table provides a concrete reversible execution engine: update the work tape, transfer the updated left word, append the instruction record below its delimiter, restore the work word, and remove the return marker. Its inverse distinctions apply to arbitrary tails, including configurations outside the initialized execution. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constructed particle events use exact positional storage. The paper supplies no uniform finite-precision tolerance that would make an approximate game or simulation trajectory a reliable halting detector. |
| tools | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | An effective field evaluator can represent the complete finite branch geometry, construct smooth cutoffs, and evaluate the prescribed force and requested mixed derivatives with derivative error bounds. This gives a concrete analytical field-generation tool whose evaluation does not depend on predicting the selected computation. |
| algorithms | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The previous-tag processor gives a finite algorithm for compiling a deterministic machine into an injective partial prefix table. It includes an input-loading rule, records the previous update tag, scans and restores the left work word, and then performs the next tape update. |
| data_structures | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The right-word processor provides a concrete tape representation with an infinite left stack, a finite right work word, a delimiter, and retained history. Its update explicitly appends a blank before restoring the right word, so a right move that empties the current work word still leaves a defined scanned cell. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite machine is represented through smooth continuum fields and exact material trajectories. No circuit layout, accelerator execution model, or physical precision contract is supplied for realizing that computation in hardware. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The histories deliberately retain overwritten instruction information and support inverse reconstruction. The paper supplies no secrecy property, adversarial model, or cryptographic construction for those records. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The field prescription is derived from a specified deterministic transition table and finite input. The paper provides no learning objective, training procedure, or statistical generalization contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The storage construction gives an explicit feasible evacuation, scaling, and delivery route with detector avoidance. It supplies no optimization objective or proof that its slot schedule, path length, support size, or storage placement is optimal. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The processors use deterministic controls and exact prefix guards with separately disjoint source and image cylinders. No communication protocol, concurrent execution semantics, or recovery construction for delayed or lost updates is provided. |
| other (Effective fluid-field synthesis and exact computation in dynamical systems) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The constructions compile complete prefix branches into smooth incompressible motions and then form a prescribed Navier–Stokes residual force. Normal compensation realizes area-changing maps on neighborhoods of coding sheets or boxes; the invariant-plane lift instead preserves the specified planar dynamics. Initialization and continuous-time detector bounds complete the exact computation-to-particle-event contract. |

          "exact_quote": "Let $B_i$ and $C_i$, $1\\le i\\le N$, be two separately positively separated finite families of nondegenerate rational closed rectangles in $(0,1)^2$. Let $F_i:B_i\\to C_i$ be the positive diagonal affine map, and let $z_0\\in(0,1)$ be rational. There is an effective smooth divergence-free field supported in $(0,1)\\times(0,1)^3$ whose time-one map agrees on a positive three-dimensional neighborhood of each $B_i\\times\\{z_0\\}$ with a diagonal affine map restricting to the map $(x,z_0)\\mapsto(F_i(x),z_0)$, with normal scale reciprocal to its planar area multiplier. On the sheet, each horizontal coordinate is constant during ascent and descent and is a convex combination of its two endpoint coordinates during the middle stage. The same construction applies in a larger bounded Euclidean region after translations and a choice of separated heights.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-sa-sheet-routing"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-invariant-lift",
          "exact_quote": "Let $b(t,r)$ be an effective smooth planar field, periodic on $\\mathbb T^2$ or compactly supported in $\\mathbb R^2$, with bounded mixed derivatives. Let $h$ be smooth effective with bounded derivatives, $h(z_0)=0$ and $h'(z_0)=1$. Then $$\\label{eq:sa-lift}\n U(t,r,z)=(h'(z)b_1,h'(z)b_2,-h(z)\\operatorname{div}_r b)$$ is divergence free and carries exactly the planar flow on $z=z_0$. The choice $h(z)=\\sin(2\\pi z)/(2\\pi)$ for $z_0=0$ gives a mean-zero unit-torus field. The choice $h=(z-z_0)\\chi(z)$, with $\\chi=1$ near $z_0$ and compact support, gives compact support on $\\mathbb R^3$ when $b$ is compactly supported, or a mean-zero torus field when $h$ is supported in one vertical chart. Time periods and mixed-derivative bounds are preserved.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-sa-invariant-lift"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-sa-realization",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U$ be an effective smooth divergence-free field, $U(0)=0$, on the unit torus or with fixed compact spatial support on $\\mathbb R^3$. Assume its mixed derivatives are bounded on each finite time interval. Then $$\\label{eq:sa-residual}\n f=\\partial_tU+(U\\cdot\\nabla)U-\\nu\\Delta U$$ is an effective prescribed force and $(u,p)=(U,0)$ is a global solution. Its velocity is unique in the applicable comparison classes of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:b-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:b-comparison\">[lem:b-comparison]</a>; pressure is determined with exactly the normalization or additive time function specified there. Uniform mixed-derivative bounds, a time period, fixed compact support, and spatial mean zero pass from $U$ to $f$ when imposed on $U$. The material flow is defined for every finite time.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-sa-realization"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-projection-l2",
          "exact_quote": "An effective smooth mean-zero force $g$ on $\\mathbb T_L^3$ has an effective mean-zero potential $\\phi$ satisfying $\\Delta\\phi=\\operatorname{div}g$. Replacing $(g,p)$ by $(g-\\nabla\\phi,p-\\phi)$ preserves velocity and makes the force solenoidal. Uniform mixed-derivative bounds, temporal $L^2$ bounds of their spatial suprema, and separately imposed time periodicity, eventual stationarity, or orderwise decay bounds are preserved.",
          "source_object": "stmt-prop-projection-l2"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
